The design answer: **the token never enters the LLM's world; a user identifier does.**\n\nFlow (user's framing, 2026-07-09):\n\n1. The user authenticates to **our own service** (the security app).\n2. The service issues a **JWT** for the user.\n3. The user's **UUID and the JWT go into a vault** — the token is stored, not circulated.\n4. From that point on, only the **bare user identifier** is passed around (e.g. into MCP tool-call context).\n5. When a tool actually needs access, a **separate process — the security app, not the tool** — receives the user id, retrieves the token from the vault, retrieves roles, determines what the requested resource allows, and returns **only the applicable roles/capabilities** back to the tool.\n\n**Enforcement principle (inherited from ASR-002):** authorization must gate what the backend *retrieves* (scoped queries), not filter results after the fact based on client-held role information.\n\n**Why statelessness fits:** the adopted MCP mechanism (ASR-003) is Anthropic's native Claude API connector — stateless, no server-side session, per-call authorization only. A stable bare user id as the per-call credential is exactly what a stateless design requires; the vault + separate resolver process supplies the state that the connector can't hold.\n\n## Threat Model — Concrete Credential-Surfacing Paths\n\n(2026-08-18) Names the specific paths \"credentials surfaced\" covers — the gap the ecosystem check flagged (its sources named the exposure-*class*, not this design's specific paths). Each path pairs with the design requirement that defends against it; several of these requirements are now committed in Key Decisions above.\n\n**Assets to protect:** the JWT signing secret; the per-user vault token (the post-auth JWT stored against the user's UUID); any downstream credential the resolver derives on the user's behalf (a scoped DB connection, a third-party API key).\n\n**Trust boundary:** the LLM's context window — conversation history, tool-call arguments, tool-call results, anything that ends up prompt-cached or in a stored transcript — must never contain an asset above. Everything server-side of an MCP tool call (resolver, vault, database) is trusted; anything that crosses into a message Claude reads is not.\n\n| # | Exposure path | Concrete scenario | Defense |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Tool-result echo | A tool handler bug (or a debug/verbose mode left on) returns the resolved token instead of derived roles/capabilities | `IAuthorizationProvider`'s return type is a typed roles/capabilities model (Pydantic/dataclass) with no token-shaped field — not a dict that could accidentally carry one |\n| 2 | Tool-call argument | A tool signature includes a `user_id` (or worse, a credential) parameter the model fills in — model-visible and model-settable | Don't pass user id as a tool argument at all — resolve it server-side per request from the verified access token before the tool body runs (committed above) |\n| 3 | Confused deputy via #2 | If #2 isn't fixed, a prompt-injected instruction (from blog/document content the model is reasoning over) tricks the model into passing a *different* user id, and the resolver serves another user's data | Same fix as #2 — this is the sharper reason to close it, not just a context-window nicety |\n| 4 | Error/exception bubbling | A vault-lookup or resolver exception's message/stack trace carries token material and becomes (or is embedded in) the tool's error response, which MCP returns as model-readable content | Resolver/vault code catches its own exceptions and translates to a generic, tokenless error before it can become a tool result — never let a raw exception cross that boundary |\n| 5 | Logging | Request/response logging (FastAPI, MCP server, connector-facing endpoint) logs `authorization_token` or vault contents; those logs later surface back to Claude via a log-search or debugging tool | Log user id, never token values — redact `authorization_token` and any vault-returned field at the logging layer itself, not by convention at each call site |\n| 6 | Prompt injection / tool poisoning | Content the model reasons over (a subscribed blog post, an uploaded document) contains an injected instruction trying to get a tool to dump credentials, or to misuse a legitimate tool's parameters | OWASP MCP standard mitigation: narrow tool schemas (no free-text \"run this query\" shape) — and #1–#4 mean there's nothing credential-shaped for an injected instruction to successfully request even if it tries |\n| 7 | Connector-level token visibility | Whether `mcp_servers[].authorization_token` (an API *request* parameter per Anthropic's docs, not a `messages` content block) ever surfaces in the model's context or a stored transcript | Documented as outside the content window by the API's shape — but **verify empirically once the POC has a running connector call**: inspect an actual transcript/prompt-cache entry, don't take the docs' word alone |\n| 8 | State-handle hijacking (MCP spec's term) | MCP is stateless, no protocol-level session; if the resolver's roles/capabilities output were treated as proof of identity, it could be replayed outside the request it was issued for | Bind every resolver output to that request's verified user id server-side; never accept a roles/capabilities value handed back as identity proof — only the verified access token counts |\n| 9 | Persistence / blast radius | If any of #1–#8 leaks a token, it now also lives in whatever stores the conversation (client-side chat history in the researcher/writer UI, prompt cache), extending exposure past the single request | A consequence of getting #1–#8 right, not a separate control — named so it's clear \"don't log it\" alone isn't sufficient once transcripts are persisted |\n\n**Resolves the open question \"how does the user id reach the tool\":** it doesn't reach the tool as a value at all — it's resolved server-side, per request, from the verified access token, invisible to the model. See the corresponding Key Decision above.\n\n## Integration target: `ai-research-blog`\n\n- **Repo:** `/Users/billkratochvil/Dev/repos/ai-research-blog` (`BillKrat/ai-research-blog`) — AI-assisted multi-tenant blog platform: Next.js UI, Python/FastAPI API, PostgreSQL triple store, deployed on Railway (blogresearch.net).\n- **Architecture:** MVPVM pattern (ADR-0002) adapted for request-scoped HTTP; two packages split by reusability — `shared/` (reusable framework) vs `blogresearch/` (app-specific, ADR-0009); small registry-based composition root; **no DI framework, no ORM**.\n- **Most relevant docs — read before designing against this POC:**\n  - `docs/ASR.md` → **ASR-002** (security as the cross-cutting foundation; JWT-based identity; token-in-vault; bare user id across process boundaries) and **ASR-003** (MCP as the tool-layer gateway: one Python MCP server, multiple tools, native Claude API connector, OAuth 2.1 / RFC 8707 for client↔server auth).\n  - `docs/adr/0005-identity-data-in-triple-store.md` — identity/role/group/feature data lives in the **same triple store** as form data (point lookups are fine for triples), accessed via `IUserProvider`; the token-vault resolution is `IAuthorizationProvider`, a deliberately separate service-layer interface.\n  - `docs/adr/0003-provider-interface-split.md` — the one-responsibility-per-interface discipline this codebase follows.\n- **Planned interfaces to align with:** `IUserProvider` (data: role/group/feature triples for a user) and `IAuthorizationProvider` (service: takes user id + resource, resolves credentials, returns roles/capabilities). That codebase keeps the C#-style `I` prefix on interface names — match it so the port is frictionless.\n- **Inherited working style** (from that repo's AGENTS.md): user is experienced in .NET/C#, learning Python; requests arrive in .NET terms but the goal is idiomatic Python. Don't import .NET ceremony — no interface for a single implementation with no near-term second one, no deep DAL/BLL/Repository stacks for something small, no frameworks the task doesn't need.\n- **Inherited \"what to avoid\":** no DI framework/ORM/heavy dependencies; no import-time side effects outside explicit entrypoints; no app code aware of the test framework; **never hardcode credentials or connection strings** — absolute here, this *is* the security system.\n- **Extraction note:** when code leaves the POC for `ai-research-blog`, it will follow that repo's `shared/` vs `blogresearch/` boundary and ADR conventions. The layer split still stands: the **MCP auth spec / `mcp` SDK** covers *client↔MCP-server* authentication (OAuth 2.1) and says nothing about *per-user, in-server* authorization — that second half is ours (ASR-002). Versions below are point-in-time snapshots; re-verify before scaffolding if a library has moved on. **Re-verified 2026-08-18 from source (tag v2.0.0) immediately before scaffolding — no drift;** the confirmed API surface is recorded in the Key Decisions row above.\n\nThe \"who's done the heavy lifting\" question resolves layer by layer:\n\n| Layer | What it does | Verified state (2026-08-18) |\n|-------|--------------|-----------------------------|\n| MCP client↔server auth | OAuth 2.1, token verification in the server | `mcp` Python SDK **2.0.0** (released 2026-07-28 — current stable; v1.x now security-patch-only): `mcp.server.auth` `TokenVerifier` + `AuthSettings` (FastMCP is now `MCPServer`). Current state of `fastapi-users` / `authlib` for the identity half.\n\n**Prejudgment to test → verified 2026-08-18: confirmed, with one correction.** *Compose standard parts + one small custom component* holds — OAuth 2.1 verification on the MCP server comes from the SDK (`TokenVerifier`), identity from `PyJWT`, and the per-user vault + capability resolver is the one genuinely custom piece. The *other* branch of the prejudgment did **not** fire: the Claude API MCP connector requires the consumer to supply and refresh the token per call (item 2) — it manages nothing on the user's behalf — so the self-issued JWT + vault + resolver is **not** simplified away. It stays exactly as the 10k-foot design has it, with the resolver's output landing in `mcp_servers[].authorization_token` on each Messages API call.\n\n### Verification results (2026-08-18)\n\n**1. `mcp` Python SDK** — `mcp` **2.0.0** on PyPI (released 2026-07-28): v2 is the current stable line — a major rework built for the **2026-07-28 spec revision** (and every earlier one); v1.x continues on a frozen line receiving security patches only, documented at `py.sdk.modelcontextprotocol.io/v1/`. `FastMCP` is renamed `MCPServer` (import `mcp.server`); `get_context()` is gone — declare a `ctx: Context` parameter instead. **Statelessness: first-class** — the 2026-07-28 spec removes the connection handshake, the session, and every server-initiated request, and `mcp.run(transport=\"streamable-http\", stateless_http=True)` gives \"a fresh transport per request, no session tracking\" (plus `json_response=True`, `event_store`, and `transport_security` for DNS-rebinding protection when deployed off-localhost). **Auth: the resource-server half of OAuth 2.1** — implement `TokenVerifier` (one async method: raw token in → `AccessToken | None` out; the SDK has no opinion on what a valid token is) together with `AuthSettings(issuer_url, resource_server_url, required_scopes)`; the SDK auto-serves RFC 9728 protected-resource metadata at `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/...` and answers unauthenticated requests with a 401 whose `WWW-Authenticate` points at it; the repo ships an RFC 7662 `IntrospectionTokenVerifier` example as the production shape. The legacy embedded authorization server (`auth_server_provider=`) is explicitly not recommended for new servers. **How per-request context reaches handlers:** `get_access_token()` (`mcp.server.auth.middleware.auth_context`) returns the current request's `AccessToken` — `client_id`, `subject`, `scopes`, `expires_at`, plus any extra claims the verifier attached — inside any tool/resource/prompt handler via a per-request context variable; the user UUID rides in as a JWT claim decoded by *our* verifier, with zero plumbing. Caveat: auth exists only on the HTTP transports — over stdio or in memory it is always `None` (the stdio security boundary is the launching process). Sources: pypi.org/project/mcp; `py.sdk.modelcontextprotocol.io` (What's new in v2, Running your server, Authorization); modelcontextprotocol/python-sdk.\n\n**2. Native Claude API MCP connector** — the Messages API \"MCP connector\" feature; current beta header `mcp-client-2025-11-20` (the 2025-04-04 header is deprecated). Available on Claude API, Claude Platform on AWS, and Microsoft Foundry (all beta). **Exact auth mechanics (the design-critical answer):** each `mcp_servers` entry takes `type: \"url\"` (must start with https), `name`, and an optional `authorization_token` — and the official docs state: \"API consumers are expected to handle the OAuth flow and obtain the access token prior to making the API call, and to refresh the token as needed.\" → **The connector carries a per-user token that *we* supply per call; it does NOT do user-scoped OAuth on the user's behalf in the API path.** (The claude.ai *product* connector is a different mechanism — consent-gated OAuth, `oauth_anthropic_creds`, no client_credentials — and is not this POC's target; ASR-003 targets the API.) Constraints that shape the POC: the server must be publicly exposed over HTTP (Streamable HTTP or SSE; no stdio); **only tool calls** travel through the connector (no prompts or resources); multiple servers per request is supported; per-tool allowlist / denylist / `defer_loading` live in `mcp_toolset` objects in the `tools` array; the Batches API accepts `mcp_servers` too; and **MCP-connector data is not covered by ZDR** (standard retention applies — note this when scoping what the tools can return). The in-SDK alternative for local/stdio servers — `anthropic[mcp]` client-side helpers (`anthropic.lib.tools.mcp`) — is ASR-003's \"hand-rolled client/loop in this app's own backend\" option, now a one-import path. Source: platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/mcp-connector; claude.com/docs/connectors/building/authentication.\n\n**3. Published security guidance (feeds the threat-model step)** — the canonical sources found: (a) the **MCP spec's \"Security Best Practices\"** document (2026-07-28 revision; modelcontextprotocol.io) — names, at MUST level: the confused deputy (proxy static client id + consent-cookie theft), **token passthrough (explicitly forbidden — \"MCP servers MUST NOT accept any tokens that were not explicitly issued for the MCP server\"; audience validation per RFC 9068)**, SSRF via OAuth discovery URLs (client side) and via CIMD (server side), **state-handle hijacking** (\"MCP is stateless and has no protocol-level sessions\" — servers MUST NOT treat possession of a state handle as authentication; bind handles server-side to the user id *derived from the verified token*), local MCP server compromise (malicious startup commands, DNS rebinding against localhost), OAuth authorization URL validation (javascript:/shell handling → XSS/RCE), stdio-proxy privilege escalation, mix-up attacks (RFC 9207), localhost redirect impersonation, CIMD trust policies, and progressive least-privilege scope minimization. (b) The **OWASP MCP Security Cheat Sheet** — the prompt-injection / tool-poisoning class plus the 2025 incident record (Asana cross-tenant data exposure, GitHub MCP prompt injection, unauthenticated RCE in Anthropic's own MCP Inspector, npm supply-chain compromises). (c) **Anthropic-specific:** no dedicated Anthropic blog post naming MCP credential exposure paths was found; the closest official guidance is the Claude Code security doc — prompt-injection safeguards (permission system, isolated context windows for web fetch, input sanitization, network-command approval), trust verification for new MCP servers, and the key precedent for this POC's core move: in Anthropic-hosted cloud sessions, \"authentication is handled through a secure proxy that uses a scoped credential inside the sandbox, which is then translated to your actual GitHub authentication token\" — i.e. *a user identifier enters the agent's world; the credential does not*. **Gap to close in the threat-model step:** neither source fully covers the POC's specific concern — a self-issued vault token echoed into model context via tool output, model-visible parameters, or logs. The vault + bare-user-id design is the defense for exactly that; name those paths explicitly in the concrete threat model.\n\n**4. Identity libraries (the \"batteries-included\" candidates)** — `fastapi-users` **15.0.5** (released 2026-03-27): **maintenance mode** — \"security updates and dependency maintenance, no new features\"; the maintainers are building \"a new Python authentication toolkit that will ultimately supersede FastAPI Users\" (unreleased). Feature set otherwise unchanged (register/login/reset/verify routes, social OAuth2, pluggable JWT/database/Redis strategies, SQLAlchemy-async/Beanie). `PyJWT` **2.13.0** (2026-05-21): active, production/stable, trusted publishing with sigstore attestation. `authlib` **1.7.2** (2026-05-06; signed releases; v1.7.0 landed 2026-04): active, full OAuth 2 + OpenID Connect client *and* server, joserfc-based, Python 3.10–3.14. `hvac` **2.4.0** (2025-10-30): active HashiCorp Vault API client, Apache-2.0. **POC selection:** `PyJWT` for the self-issued JWT half — smallest footprint, matches ASR-002's self-contained-JWT lean, and makes no bet on a superseded library; hold `authlib` in reserve if the MCP server ever needs its own interactive authorization server (e.g. for non-API clients); the vault stays a Postgres table behind an interface at POC scale, with `hvac` as the escape hatch if a real vault is wanted.\n\nTwo design consequences to carry into the open questions (both follow from items 1 + 2, not from the POC's own reasoning): (1) the self-issued JWT must be one the MCP server's `TokenVerifier` can validate — audience bound to the MCP server's endpoint, per the spec's token-passthrough rule (RFC 9068); and (2) the backend needs a token-lifecycle path — re-fetch or re-mint from the vault on 401 — because the connector consumes one token per call and the spec's stateless posture gives it nothing else. Also, when scaffolding: pin `mcp>=2.0,<3` (v2 is three weeks old at verification time) and read the migration guide before writing any v1-style (`FastMCP`) code.\n\n## Collaboration & Communication Style\n\n- **User background:** Experienced C# developer, new to Python.\n- **Motivation:** Adopting Python after the new (stateless) Anthropic MCP SDK — with no foreseeable C# support.\n- **Communication:** The user describes requirements and concepts in **C# terms**. Translate the intent, then express it idiomatically in Python.\n- **Do NOT** write \"C# in Python clothing\" — no `foreach`-style habits, verbose property boilerplate, or ported C# patterns. Follow Python best practices in all areas:\n  - PEP 8 style; `snake_case` for variables/functions/modules, `PascalCase` for classes, `UPPER_SNAKE_CASE` for constants\n  - Dataclasses / `TypedDict` over C#-style classes for plain data; `Optional[T]`/`X | None` typing\n  - `pathlib.Path` over raw strings; `with` statements for resources\n  - Comprehensions and generator expressions where readable\n  - `black` formatting, `ruff` linting, `mypy` for type checking (or agreed equivalents)\n  - `pytest` for tests (matches the target project)\n- **Teaching moments:** When a Python idiom replaces a C# pattern the user mentioned, briefly note the correspondence (e.g., \"C# `IEnumerable<T>` + LINQ → Python generator + comprehension\") so the user can learn from the review. Keep notes short; don't over-explain basics an experienced developer doesn't need.\n- **Type hints on everything** public; this is the closest thing to C#'s static typing and helps the user's review.\n- **One exception to pure-Python naming:** the target codebase keeps the C# `I` prefix on interface names (`IUserProvider`, `IAuthorizationProvider`). Match that when using those names; everything else stays conventionally Pythonic.\n\n## Local LM Studio Agent — Role & Workflow\n\n- **Why:** Peak Claude sessions are budget-constrained (usage + provider costs already eat into it). A local model in LM Studio (currently `qwen/qwen3.8-27b` via MLX, with `mcp/filesystem` and — once its MCP config is stable — web search) absorbs legwork between peak sessions, so peak time isn't spent on things a local model can do.\n- **Division of labor:**\n  - **Local LM Studio agent:** repo exploration (`list_directory` / `search_files` / `read_text_file`), the \"Ecosystem Check → Verification list\" research above, drafting scaffolding/docs/code in this POC repo.\n  - **Claude (peak sessions):** design decisions, architecture calls, and the **review/approval gate** — nothing produced here moves into `ai-research-blog` without a Claude session reviewing it first.\n- **Recording results:** when the local agent finishes a verification item or makes a finding, log it in this file (the Key Decisions table, or directly in the relevant table/section it touches) with a date, so the next peak session picks up from there instead of re-deriving it. (Precedent set 2026-08-18: the ecosystem-check verification ran in a Claude session — web search / fetch MCP tools available — and its results were logged the same way.)\n- **Guardrail:** this repo (`poc/`) is the sandbox. Nothing here is \"done\" until a Claude session has reviewed it and deliberately moved it into `ai-research-blog` — the local agent's output is a draft/proposal, not a merge-ready artifact.\n- **Known capability gap (found 2026-08-18, first full scaffolding run) — closed same day.** The local agent's toolset (`mcp/filesystem` + a web-fetch tool + a JS-only sandbox with no shell/`--allow-run` access) had **no way to execute Python or run `pytest`.** It confirmed this itself mid-session (reasoned through and ruled out `run_javascript`/Deno as a path to a shell) and correctly flagged \"not executed yet\" as a caveat in its handoff — that honesty held. But it meant every test file it wrote was composed by reasoning alone, never run, and cross-file contracts it invented in the same sitting (e.g., a fake DB connection's method signature vs. the real driver's shape used elsewhere) could drift without it ever noticing. First run: 31 failed / 12 errors / 22 passed out of ~65 tests, all from this class of bug — see the Key Decisions row above.\n- **`poc-test-runner` MCP tool (added 2026-08-18):** closes the gap above. Two tools, both hardcoded to this repo only — no parameter can point them anywhere else (path-traversal / absolute-path / `~` arguments are rejected before they ever reach a subprocess; source at `~/.lmstudio/tools/poc_test_runner.py`):\n  - `sync_deps()` — runs `uv sync --extra dev` in this repo.\n  - `run_tests(pytest_args=\"\")` — runs `uv run pytest {pytest_args}` in this repo, returns stdout/stderr/returncode. Empty `pytest_args` runs the whole suite; e.g. `\"-k vault\"` or `\"tests/test_vault.py -v\"` scopes it.\n  - **Use it whenever you write or change any code in `src/` or `tests/`: call `sync_deps` once if dependencies might be stale, then `run_tests` after every change, and keep iterating until it's green — or until the tool stops you (see Blocked-Task Protocol below) — before handing back to a peak session.** Don't hand off code you haven't run through this tool — that was exactly the gap that produced the 2026-08-18 failures above.\n  - **`run_tests` has a per-session budget (12 calls / 45 minutes, added 2026-08-18)**, enforced by the tool itself, not by your judgment — it starts warning at call 9 and stops running pytest entirely past call 12 or 45 minutes, returning a message pointing back here. This exists because nothing else bounds how long a fix-and-rerun loop can run once a failure turns out to be genuinely hard, and a long session is exactly where prose instructions like \"stop if you're stuck\" tend to get lost. Budget it accordingly: don't burn 5 calls re-running the whole suite to check one line of a fix — use `pytest_args` to scope to just the failing test(s) while iterating, and only run the full suite once you think you're done or once per few fixes.\n\n  ### Blocked-Task Protocol\n  Triggered when `run_tests` refuses to run (budget exceeded), or on your own judgment if a specific failure has survived 2-3 real fix attempts. Stop touching that test. In this file, under the relevant Key Decisions or Current State entry, record for each test still failing:\n  - **Test name** (e.g. `tests/test_vault.py::test_put_upserts`)\n  - **Exact error/traceback** from your last `run_tests` call — not a paraphrase\n  - **What you tried** — each attempt and why it didn't work\n  - **Your best hypothesis** for the remaining cause, even if unproven\n\n  Then move on: fix what you can elsewhere in the suite, and leave the rest for peak-session review. A partial fix with an honest, detailed write-up on the remainder is far more useful than a full session spent on one stubborn test with nothing to show for it.\n\n## Conventions\n\n- Language: Python 3.12+ (the POC requires `>=3.12`; the target repo runs 3.13.7 — compatible)\n- Styling: `black` + `ruff` (line length 100); types enforced with `mypy` (config in pyproject; untyped third-party deps overridden there)\n- Tests: `pytest`, in `tests/` mirroring source layout. DB-dependent components are tested against an in-memory fake connection via the injectable `connect` parameter (the target repo's pattern) — the suite needs zero infrastructure; `test_integration.py` covers the real-Postgres path and skips unless the POC env vars are set\n- Env/secrets: `.env` (gitignored) + committed `.env.example` with placeholders only — same pattern as the target repo; **never a real secret in a committed file**. Entry points (not library modules) call `load_dotenv()` — the target repo's no-import-time-side-effects discipline\n- Package layout: one flat `src/mcpauth` package (hatchling, src layout) — held loose until the deployment topology is confirmed (docs/0001); module boundaries kept extraction-clean so the port to `ai-research-blog` is a move\n- Naming: the target repo's `I` prefix applies to `IUserProvider` / `IAuthorizationProvider`; everything else conventionally Pythonic (no interface for a single implementation — the vault and identity anchor are concrete on purpose)\n\n## Current State & Next Steps\n\n- [x] Set project context (this file): goal, 10k-foot design, integration target\n- [x] Align with `ai-research-blog` docs (ASR-002/003, ADR-0005)\n- [x] Verify current ecosystem state (see Ecosystem Check) — done 2026-08-18 in a Claude session with web access\n- [x] Concrete threat model — done 2026-08-18, see Threat Model section\n- [x] Decide JWT shape — self-contained, committed 2026-08-18\n- [x] Decide vault implementation at POC scale — Postgres table, committed 2026-08-18\n- [x] Define the identity anchor shape — fixed anchor + triples, committed 2026-08-18\n- [ ] Confirm deployment topology — decision brief in docs/0001 (A/B/C + the tied signing-algorithm decision); Now, the fix to `_FakeConnection`:

```python
class _FakeConnection:
    """Enough of psycopg2's surface for `with closing(connect(...))` + `cursor()`.

    Matches the real driver's two-step shape: `cur = conn.cursor()`,
    then `cur.execute(sql, params)`.
    """

    def __init__(self, db: FakeDb) -&gt; None:
        self._db = db
        self._last_result: list[tuple] = []

    def cursor(self) -&gt; "_FakeCursor":
        return _FakeCursor(self._db)

    def commit(self) -&gt; None:
        pass

    def rollback(self) -&gt; None:
        pass

    def close(self) -&gt; None:
        pass
```

Interesting — `test_integration.py` uses `init_schema()`, which calls `psycopg2.connect` directly. When env vars are set (in the `settings` fixture in conftest, `DATABASE_URL` is set to `postgres://fake`!), `_env_ready()` returns True, and the integration tests will actually try to connect to the real database!

Wait, this is a problem: conftest's `settings` fixture sets `DATABASE_URL=postgres://fake` via monkeypatch. But the `needs_env` marker is evaluated at module import time, when `.env` is loaded (conftest calls `load_dotenv()` at import time). If `.env` has DATABASE_URL etc. set, then even without the fixture, `_env_ready()` will be True.

### Issue 5: `httpx.Client` over `ASGITransport` — async only

`httpx.ASGITransport` is async-only (implements `handle_async_request`). A sync `httpx.Client` using `ASGITransport` fails with `AttributeError: 'ASGITransport' object has no attribute 'handle_request'`.

Fix per AGENTS.md: "switch to `httpx.AsyncClient` + `pytest-asyncio` (or sync `TestClient`)".

Two choices:
1. `httpx.AsyncClient(transport=ASGITransport(...))` + `pytest-asyncio` — requires adding pytest-asyncio to pyproject dev deps, and either decorating all tests in these two files as async, or using a fixture that runs the requests in an event loop.
2. Starlette's `TestClient` (`from starlette.testclient import TestClient`) — sync, wraps httpx with a portal that runs the ASGI app in a background thread. TestClient is a subclass of `httpx.Client`! So `TestClient(app, base_url=...)` behaves like httpx.Client for the test's call style (`.post(..., json=...)`, `.get(...)`, `.headers`, `.text`, `.json()`).

Option 2 is much less invasive: just change `_client` in test_auth_app.py and the mcp_env fixture in test_mcp_server.py to use `TestClient` instead of `httpx.Client(transport=...)`. TestClient comes with starlette (a fastapi dep), no new dep required.

A user can hold\n--    SEVERAL roles, which is the multi-valued case, so the POC relaxes\n--    the uniqueness to (subject, predicate, object_value). Authorization Grant (ID-JAG) for an access token.\n\n        This is leg 2 of SEP-990: the client presents an ID-JAG - issued by the enterprise\n        identity provider - using the RFC 7523 [{"type":"text","text":"import contextvars\n\nfrom starlette.types import ASGIApp, Receive, Scope, Send\n\nfrom mcp.server.auth.middleware.bearer_auth import AuthenticatedUser\nfrom mcp.server.auth.provider import AccessToken\n\n# Create a contextvar to store the authenticated user\n# The default is None, indicating no authenticated user is present\nauth_context_var = contextvars.ContextVar[AuthenticatedUser | None](\"auth_context\", default=None)\n\n\ndef get_access_token() -> AccessToken | None:\n    \"\"\"Get the access token from the current context.\n\n    Returns:\n        The access token if an authenticated user is available, None otherwise.\n    \"\"\"\n    auth_user = auth_context_var.get()\n    return auth_user.access_token if auth_user else None\n\n\nclass AuthContextMiddleware:\n    \"\"\"Middleware that extracts the authenticated user from the request\n    and sets it in a contextvar for easy access throughout the request lifecycle.\n\n    This middleware should be added after the AuthenticationMiddleware in the\n    middleware stack to ensure that the user is properly authenticated before\n    being stored in the context.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, app: ASGIApp):\n        self.app = app\n\n    async def __call__(self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send):\n        user = scope.get(\"user\")\n        if isinstance(user, AuthenticatedUser):\n            # Set the authenticated user in the contextvar\n            token = auth_context_var.set(user)\n            try:\n                await self.app(scope, receive, send)\n            finally:\n                auth_context_var.reset(token)\n        else:\n            # No authenticated user, just process the request\n            await self.app(scope, receive, send)\n"}]

constant).\"\"\"\n\nAPP_MIME_TYPE = \"text/html;profile=mcp-app\"\n\"\"\"MIME type for a `ui://` app resource.\"\"\"\n\nVisibility = Literal[\"model\", \"app\"]\n\"\"\"Where a UI-bound tool is list[str] | None = None\n    frame_domains: list[str] | None = None\n    base_uri_domains: list[str] | None = None\n\n\nclass Apps(Extension):\n    \"\"\"The MCP Apps extension: bind tools to `ui://` \"\"\"\n\n    identifier = EXTENSION_ID\n\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\n        self._tools: list[tuple[ToolBinding, str]] = []  # (binding, bound resource_uri)\n        self._resources: None,\n        **tool_kwargs: Any,\n    ) -> Callable[[_CallableT], _CallableT]:\n        \"\"\"Decorator registering a tool bound to a `ui://` resource.\n\n        Stamps `_meta.ui.resourceUri` (and kwargs=tool_kwargs)\n            self._tools.append((binding, resource_uri))\n            return fn\n\n        return decorator\n\n    def add_html_resource(\n        self,\n        uri: str,\n        html: str,\n        *,\n        name: str | None = None,\n        title: str | None = None,\n        description: str | None = None,\n        csp: ResourceCsp | None = None,\n        permissions: add_resource(self, resource: Resource) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Register a pre-built `ui://` resource.\n\n        The escape hatch for resources `add_html_resource` cannot express (e.g. a\n overload\n\nimport anyio\nimport pydantic_core\nfrom mcp_types import (\n    INTERNAL_ERROR,\n    INVALID_PARAMS,\n    METHOD_NOT_FOUND,\n    MISSING_REQUIRED_CLIENT_CAPABILITY,\n    Annotations,\n    BlobResourceContents,\n    CallToolRequestParams,\n    CallToolResult,\n    ClientCapabilities,\n    CompleteRequestParams,\n    CompleteResult,\n    Completion,\n    GetPromptRequestParams,\n    GetPromptResult,\n    Icon,\n    InputRequiredResult,\n    ListPromptsResult,\n    ListResourcesResult,\n    ListResourceTemplatesResult,\n    ListToolsResult,\n    MissingRequiredClientCapabilityErrorData,\n    PaginatedRequestParams,\n    ReadResourceRequestParams,\n    ReadResourceResult,\n    TextContent,\n    TextResourceContents,\n    ToolAnnotations,\n)\nfrom mcp_types import Prompt as MCPPrompt\nfrom mcp_types import PromptArgument as MCPPromptArgument\nfrom mcp_types import Resource as MCPResource\nfrom mcp_types import ResourceTemplate as MCPResourceTemplate\nfrom mcp_types import Tool as MCPTool\nfrom pydantic import BaseModel\nfrom pydantic.networks import AnyUrl\nfrom starlette.applications import Starlette\nfrom starlette.middleware import Middleware\nfrom starlette.middleware.authentication import AuthenticationMiddleware\nfrom starlette.requests import Request\nfrom starlette.responses import Response\nfrom starlette.routing import Mount, Route\nfrom starlette.types import Receive, Scope, Send\n\nfrom mcp.server.auth.middleware.auth_context import AuthContextMiddleware\nfrom mcp.server.auth.middleware.bearer_auth import BearerAuthBackend, RequireAuthMiddleware\nfrom mcp.server.auth.provider import OAuthAuthorizationServerProvider, ProviderTokenVerifier, TokenVerifier\nfrom mcp.server.auth.settings import AuthSettings\nfrom mcp.server.caching import CacheableMethod, CacheHint\nfrom mcp.server.context import HandlerResult, ServerMiddleware, ServerRequestContext\nfrom mcp.server.extension import (\n    Extension,\n    MethodBinding,\n    RequestHandler,\n    compose_tool_call_handler,\n    validate_extension_identifier,\n)\nfrom mcp.server.lowlevel.helper_types import ReadResourceContents\nfrom mcp.server.lowlevel.server import LifespanResultT, Server\nfrom mcp.server.lowlevel.server import lifespan as default_lifespan\nfrom mcp.server.mcpserver.context import Context\nfrom mcp.server.mcpserver.exceptions import ResourceError, ResourceNotFoundError\nfrom mcp.server.mcpserver.prompts import Prompt, PromptManager\nfrom mcp.server.mcpserver.resources import (\n    DEFAULT_RESOURCE_SECURITY,\n    FunctionResource,\n    Resource,\n    ResourceManager,\n    ResourceSecurity,\n)\nfrom mcp.server.mcpserver.tools import Tool, ToolManager\nfrom mcp.server.mcpserver.utilities.context_injection import find_context_parameter\nfrom mcp.server.mcpserver.utilities.logging import configure_logging, get_logger\nfrom mcp.server.request_state import RequestStateBoundary, RequestStateSecurity\nfrom mcp.server.sse import SseServerTransport\nfrom mcp.server.stdio import stdio_server\nfrom mcp.server.streamable_http import EventStore\nfrom mcp.server.streamable_http_manager import DEFAULT_MAX_REQUEST_BODY_SIZE, StreamableHTTPSessionManager\nfrom mcp.server.subscriptions import InMemorySubscriptionBus, ListenHandler, SubscriptionBus\nfrom mcp.server.transport_security import TransportSecuritySettings\nfrom mcp.shared.exceptions import MCPError\nfrom mcp.shared.uri_template import UriTemplate\n\nlogger = get_logger(__name__)\n\n_CallableT = TypeVar(\"_CallableT\", bound=Callable[..., Any])\n\n\nclass Settings(BaseModel, Generic[LifespanResultT]):\n    \"\"\"MCPServer settings, as passed to the `MCPServer` constructor.\"\"\"\n\n    # Server settings\n    debug: bool\n    log_level: Literal[\"DEBUG\", \"INFO\", \"WARNING\", \"ERROR\", \"CRITICAL\"]\n\n    # resource settings\n    warn_on_duplicate_resources: bool\n\n    # tool settings\n    warn_on_duplicate_tools: bool\n\n    # prompt settings\n    warn_on_duplicate_prompts: bool\n\n    dependencies: list[str]\n    \"\"\"List of dependencies to install in the server environment. Used by the `mcp install` and `mcp dev` CLI.\"\"\"\n\n    lifespan: Callable[[MCPServer[LifespanResultT]], AbstractAsyncContextManager[LifespanResultT]] | None\n    \"\"\"An async context manager that will be called when the server is started.\"\"\"\n\n    auth: AuthSettings | None\n\n\n_MISSING_AUDIENCE = (\n    \"request_state_security is configured but this server has no name. Sealed\\n\"\n    \"requestState carries the server name as an audience claim, so state minted by\\n\"\n    \"another service that shares the same keys is rejected; unnamed servers would\\n\"\n    \"all stamp the same placeholder and the check would mean nothing. Name the\\n\"\n    'server (MCPServer(\"my-service\", ...)) or set RequestStateSecurity(audience=...).'\n)\n\n\ndef lifespan_wrapper(\n    app: MCPServer[LifespanResultT],\n    lifespan: Callable[[MCPServer[LifespanResultT]], AbstractAsyncContextManager[LifespanResultT]],\n) -> Callable[[Server[LifespanResultT]], AbstractAsyncContextManager[LifespanResultT]]:\n    @asynccontextmanager\n    async def wrap(_: Server[LifespanResultT]) -> AsyncIterator[LifespanResultT]:\n        async with lifespan(app) as context:\n            yield context\n\n    return wrap\n\n\nclass MCPServer(Generic[LifespanResultT]):\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        name: str | None = None,\n        title: str | None = None,\n        description: str | None = None,\n        instructions: str | None = None,\n        website_url: str | None = None,\n        icons: list[Icon] | None = None,\n        version: str = \"\",\n        auth_server_provider: OAuthAuthorizationServerProvider[Any, Any, Any] | None = None,\n        token_verifier: TokenVerifier | None = None,\n        *,\n        tools: list[Tool] | None = None,\n        resources: list[Resource] | None = None,\n        extensions: Sequence[Extension] | None = None,\n        debug: bool = False,\n        log_level: Literal[\"DEBUG\", \"INFO\", \"WARNING\", \"ERROR\", \"CRITICAL\"] = \"INFO\",\n        warn_on_duplicate_resources: bool = True,\n        warn_on_duplicate_tools: bool = True,\n        warn_on_duplicate_prompts: bool = True,\n        dependencies: list[str] | None = None,\n        lifespan: Callable[[MCPServer[LifespanResultT]], AbstractAsyncContextManager[LifespanResultT]] | None = None,\n        auth: AuthSettings | None = None,\n        resource_security: ResourceSecurity = DEFAULT_RESOURCE_SECURITY,\n        request_state_security: RequestStateSecurity | None = None,\n        cache_hints: Mapping[CacheableMethod, CacheHint] | None = None,\n        subscriptions: SubscriptionBus | None = None,\n        middleware: Sequence[ServerMiddleware[Any]] | None = None,\n    ):\n        self._resource_security = resource_security\n        self.settings = Settings(\n            debug=debug,\n            log_level=log_level,\n            warn_on_duplicate_resources=warn_on_duplicate_resources,\n            warn_on_duplicate_tools=warn_on_duplicate_tools,\n            warn_on_duplicate_prompts=warn_on_duplicate_prompts,\n            dependencies=dependencies or [],\n            lifespan=lifespan,\n            auth=auth,\n        )\n        self.dependencies = self.settings.dependencies\n\n        self._tool_manager = ToolManager(tools=tools, warn_on_duplicate_tools=self.settings.warn_on_duplicate_tools)\n        self._resource_manager = ResourceManager(\n            resources=resources, warn_on_duplicate_resources=self.settings.warn_on_duplicate_resources\n        )\n        self._prompt_manager = PromptManager(warn_on_duplicate_prompts=self.settings.warn_on_duplicate_prompts)\n        # The subscriptions/listen fan-out seam (2026-07-28). The default bus is\n        # in-process; pass an `SubscriptionBus` implementation over an external pub/sub\n        # backend to fan events out across replicas.\n        self._subscriptions: SubscriptionBus = subscriptions if subscriptions is not None else InMemorySubscriptionBus()\n        self._lowlevel_server = Server(\n            name=name or \"mcp-server\",\n            title=title,\n            description=description,\n            instructions=instructions,\n            website_url=website_url,\n            icons=icons,\n            version=version,\n            cache_hints=cache_hints,\n            on_list_tools=self._handle_list_tools,\n            on_call_tool=self._handle_call_tool,\n            on_list_resources=self._handle_list_resources,\n            on_read_resource=self._handle_read_resource,\n            on_list_resource_templates=self._handle_list_resource_templates,\n            on_list_prompts=self._handle_list_prompts,\n            on_get_prompt=self._handle_get_prompt,\n            on_subscriptions_listen=ListenHandler(self._subscriptions),\n            # TODO(Marcelo): It seems there's a type mismatch between the lifespan type from an MCPServer and Server.\n            # We need to create a Lifespan type that is a generic on the server type, like Starlette does.\n            lifespan=(lifespan_wrapper(self, self.settings.lifespan) if self.settings.lifespan else default_lifespan),  # type: ignore\n        )\n        # Ordering: inside OpenTelemetry (spans record the sealed wire form).\n        # Extension interceptors run at the handler layer, inside this\n        # boundary, so they see plaintext.\n        if request_state_security is None:\n            security = RequestStateSecurity.ephemeral()\n        else:\n            # A supplied policy usually means shared keys, where the audience claim is\n            # what separates services; an unnamed server would stamp the placeholder.\n            if not name and request_state_security.audience is None:\n                raise ValueError(_MISSING_AUDIENCE)\n            security = request_state_security\n        self._lowlevel_server.middleware.append(RequestStateBoundary(security, default_audience=self.name))\n        # User middleware runs inside the SDK's built-ins (OpenTelemetry, then the\n        # request-state boundary), outermost-first in the order given.\n        self._lowlevel_server.middleware.extend(middleware or ())\n        # Validate auth configuration\n        if self.settings.auth is not None:\n            if auth_server_provider and token_verifier:  # pragma: no cover\n                raise ValueError(\"Cannot specify both auth_server_provider and token_verifier\")\n            if not auth_server_provider and not token_verifier:  # pragma: no cover\n                raise ValueError(\"Must specify either auth_server_provider or token_verifier when auth is enabled\")\n        elif auth_server_provider or token_verifier:\n            raise ValueError(\"Cannot specify auth_server_provider or token_verifier without auth settings\")\n\n        self._auth_server_provider = auth_server_provider\n        self._token_verifier = token_verifier\n\n        # Create token verifier from provider if needed (backwards compatibility)\n        if auth_server_provider and not token_verifier:\n            self._token_verifier = ProviderTokenVerifier(auth_server_provider)\n        self._custom_starlette_routes: list[Route] = []\n\n        # Configure logging\n        configure_logging(self.settings.log_level)\n\n        self._extensions: list[Extension] = []\n        for extension in extensions or ():\n            self._apply_extension(extension)\n        self._install_extension_interceptor()\n\n    @property\n    def name(self) -> str:\n        return self._lowlevel_server.name\n\n    @property\n    def middleware(self) -> list[ServerMiddleware[Any]]:\n        \"\"\"The middleware chain wrapping every inbound message, outermost-first.\n\n        The same list as the low-level `Server.middleware`: append an\n        `async (ctx, call_next)` callable to observe, refuse, or rewrite\n        messages before they reach a handler. Provisional - the signature is\n        expected to change before v2 is final; see the middleware guide.\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._lowlevel_server.middleware\n\n    @property\n    def title(self) -> str | None:\n        return self._lowlevel_server.title\n\n    @property\n    def description(self) -> str | None:\n        return self._lowlevel_server.description\n\n    @property\n    def instructions(self) -> str | None:\n        return self._lowlevel_server.instructions\n\n    @property\n    def website_url(self) -> str | None:\n        return self._lowlevel_server.website_url\n\n    @property\n    def icons(self) -> list[Icon] | None:\n        return self._lowlevel_server.icons\n\n    @property\n    def version(self) -> str:\n        return self._lowlevel_server.version\n\n    @property\n    def session_manager(self) -> StreamableHTTPSessionManager:\n        \"\"\"Get the StreamableHTTP session manager.\n\n        This is exposed to enable advanced use cases like mounting multiple\n        MCPServer instances in a single FastAPI application.\n\n        Raises:\n            RuntimeError: If called before streamable_http_app() has been called.\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._lowlevel_server.session_manager\n\n    def _apply_extension(self, extension: Extension) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Apply one opt-in extension's contributions through the public surface.\n\n        Registers its tools/resources/methods and advertises its settings under\n        `ServerCapabilities.extensions[extension.identifier]`. Extensions are fixed\n        at construction, so this is private; the `tools/call` interceptor is\n        composed once afterwards by `_install_extension_interceptor`.\n        \"\"\"\n        identifier = getattr(extension, \"identifier\", None)\n        validate_extension_identifier(identifier, owner=type(extension).__name__)\n        if any(e.identifier == identifier for e in self._extensions):\n            raise ValueError(f\"Extension {identifier!r} is already registered\")\n        self._extensions.append(extension)\n\n        for tool in extension.tools():\n            self.add_tool(tool.fn, meta=tool.meta, **tool.kwargs)\n        for resource in extension.resources():\n            self.add_resource(resource.resource)\n        for method in extension.methods():\n            if self._lowlevel_server.get_request_handler(method.method) is not None:\n                raise ValueError(\n                    f\"Extension {identifier!r} binds method {method.method!r}, which is already \"\n                    \"registered; extension methods are additive and cannot replace another handler\"\n                )\n            handler = _version_gated(method) if method.protocol_versions is not None else method.handler\n            self._lowlevel_server.add_request_handler(method.method, method.params_type, handler)\n\n        self._lowlevel_server.extensions[extension.identifier] = extension.settings()\n\n    def _install_extension_interceptor(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Wrap the `tools/call` handler with every extension's interceptor.\n\n        Installed only when at least one extension overrides `intercept_tool_call`,\n        so a server with purely additive extensions keeps the bare handler. The\n        chain wraps the handler itself, below the runner's outbound envelope\n        pass, so a short-circuiting interceptor's result is sieved and stamped\n        exactly like a handler result.\n        \"\"\"\n        if any(type(e).intercept_tool_call is not Extension.intercept_tool_call for e in self._extensions):\n            self._lowlevel_server.add_request_handler(\n                \"tools/call\",\n                CallToolRequestParams,\n                compose_tool_call_handler(self._extensions, self._handle_call_tool),\n            )\n\n    @overload\n    def run(self, transport: Literal[\"stdio\"] = ...) -> None: ...\n\n    @overload\n    def run(\n        self,\n        transport: Literal[\"sse\"],\n        *,\n        host: str = ...,\n        port: int = ...,\n        sse_path: str = ...,\n        message_path: str = ...,\n        transport_security: TransportSecuritySettings | None = ...,\n    ) -> None: ...\n\n    @overload\n    def run(\n        self,\n        transport: Literal[\"streamable-http\"],\n        *,\n        host: str = ...,\n        port: int = ...,\n        streamable_http_path: str = ...,\n        json_response: bool = ...,\n        stateless_http: bool = ...,\n        event_store: EventStore | None = ...,\n        retry_interval: int | None = ...,\n        max_request_body_size: int = ...,\n        transport_security: TransportSecuritySettings | None = ...,\n    ) -> None: ...\n\n    def run(\n        self,\n        transport: Literal[\"stdio\", \"sse\", \"streamable-http\"] = \"stdio\",\n        **kwargs: Any,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Run the MCP server. Note this is a synchronous function.\n\n        Args:\n            transport: Transport protocol to use (\"stdio\", \"sse\", or \"streamable-http\")\n            **kwargs: Transport-specific options (see overloads for details)\n        \"\"\"\n        TRANSPORTS = Literal[\"stdio\", \"sse\", \"streamable-http\"]\n        if transport not in TRANSPORTS.__args__:  # type: ignore  # pragma: no cover\n            raise ValueError(f\"Unknown transport: {transport}\")\n\n        match transport:\n            case \"stdio\":\n                anyio.run(self.run_stdio_async)\n            case \"sse\":  # pragma: no cover\n                anyio.run(lambda: self.run_sse_async(**kwargs))\n            case \"streamable-http\":  # pragma: no cover\n                anyio.run(lambda: self.run_streamable_http_async(**kwargs))\n\n    async def _handle_list_tools(\n        self, ctx: ServerRequestContext[LifespanResultT], params: PaginatedRequestParams | None\n    ) -> ListToolsResult:\n        return ListToolsResult(tools=await self.list_tools())\n\n    async def _handle_call_tool(\n        self, ctx: ServerRequestContext[LifespanResultT], params: CallToolRequestParams\n    ) -> CallToolResult | InputRequiredResult:\n        context = Context(request_context=ctx, mcp_server=self, input_params=params, subscriptions=self._subscriptions)\n        try:\n            return await self.call_tool(params.name, params.arguments or {}, context)\n        except MCPError:\n            raise\n        except Exception as e:\n            return CallToolResult(content=[TextContent(type=\"text\", text=str(e))], is_error=True)\n\n    async def _handle_list_resources(\n        self, ctx: ServerRequestContext[LifespanResultT], params: PaginatedRequestParams | None\n    ) -> ListResourcesResult:\n        return ListResourcesResult(resources=await self.list_resources())\n\n    async def _handle_read_resource(\n        self, ctx: ServerRequestContext[LifespanResultT], params: ReadResourceRequestParams\n    ) -> ReadResourceResult | InputRequiredResult:\n        context = Context(request_context=ctx, mcp_server=self, input_params=params, subscriptions=self._subscriptions)\n        try:\n            results = await self.read_resource(params.uri, context)\n        except ResourceNotFoundError as err:\n            raise MCPError(code=INVALID_PARAMS, message=str(err), data={\"uri\": str(params.uri)})\n        except ResourceError as err:\n            raise MCPError(code=INTERNAL_ERROR, message=str(err), data={\"uri\": str(params.uri)})\n        if isinstance(results, InputRequiredResult):\n            return results\n        contents: list[TextResourceContents | BlobResourceContents] = []\n        for item in results:\n            if isinstance(item.content, bytes):\n                contents.append(\n                    BlobResourceContents(\n                        uri=params.uri,\n                        blob=base64.b64encode(item.content).decode(),\n                        mime_type=item.mime_type or \"application/octet-stream\",\n                        _meta=item.meta,\n                    )\n                )\n            else:\n                contents.append(\n                    TextResourceContents(\n                        uri=params.uri,\n                        text=item.content,\n                        mime_type=item.mime_type or \"text/plain\",\n                        _meta=item.meta,\n                    )\n                )\n        return ReadResourceResult(contents=contents)\n\n    async def _handle_list_resource_templates(\n        self, ctx: ServerRequestContext[LifespanResultT], params: PaginatedRequestParams | None\n    ) -> ListResourceTemplatesResult:\n        return ListResourceTemplatesResult(resource_templates=await self.list_resource_templates())\n\n    async def _handle_list_prompts(\n        self, ctx: ServerRequestContext[LifespanResultT], params: PaginatedRequestParams | None\n    ) -> ListPromptsResult:\n        return ListPromptsResult(prompts=await self.list_prompts())\n\n    async def _handle_get_prompt(\n        self, ctx: ServerRequestContext[LifespanResultT], params: GetPromptRequestParams\n    ) -> GetPromptResult | InputRequiredResult:\n        context = Context(request_context=ctx, mcp_server=self, input_params=params, subscriptions=self._subscriptions)\n        return await self.get_prompt(params.name, params.arguments, context)\n\n    async def list_tools(self) -> list[MCPTool]:\n        \"\"\"List all available tools.\"\"\"\n        tools = self._tool_manager.list_tools()\n        return [\n            MCPTool(\n                name=info.name,\n                title=info.title,\n                description=info.description,\n                input_schema=info.parameters,\n                output_schema=info.output_schema,\n                annotations=info.annotations,\n                icons=info.icons,\n                _meta=info.meta,\n            )\n            for info in tools\n        ]\n\n    async def call_tool(\n        self, name: str, arguments: dict[str, Any], context: Context[LifespanResultT, Any] | None = None\n    ) -> CallToolResult | InputRequiredResult:\n        \"\"\"Call a tool by name with arguments.\"\"\"\n        if context is None:\n            context = Context(mcp_server=self, subscriptions=self._subscriptions)\n        return await self._tool_manager.call_tool(name, arguments, context, convert_result=True)\n\n    async def list_resources(self) -> list[MCPResource]:\n        \"\"\"List all available resources.\"\"\"\n\n        resources = self._resource_manager.list_resources()\n        return [\n            MCPResource(\n                uri=resource.uri,\n                name=resource.name or \"\",\n                title=resource.title,\n                description=resource.description,\n                mime_type=resource.mime_type,\n                icons=resource.icons,\n                annotations=resource.annotations,\n                _meta=resource.meta,\n            )\n            for resource in resources\n        ]\n\n    async def list_resource_templates(self) -> list[MCPResourceTemplate]:\n        templates = self._resource_manager.list_templates()\n        return [\n            MCPResourceTemplate(\n                uri_template=template.uri_template,\n                name=template.name,\n                title=template.title,\n                description=template.description,\n                mime_type=template.mime_type,\n                icons=template.icons,\n                annotations=template.annotations,\n                _meta=template.meta,\n            )\n            for template in templates\n        ]\n\n    async def read_resource(\n        self, uri: AnyUrl | str, context: Context[LifespanResultT, Any] | None = None\n    ) -> Iterable[ReadResourceContents] | InputRequiredResult:\n        \"\"\"Read a resource by URI.\n\n        An `InputRequiredResult` returned by a resource template function is\n        passed through unchanged (the 2026-07-28 multi-round-trip flow); the\n        retry's answers arrive on `ctx.input_responses`, with\n        `ctx.request_state` carrying the echoed opaque state.\n\n        Raises:\n            ResourceNotFoundError: If no resource or template matches the URI.\n            ResourceError: If template creation or resource reading fails.\n        \"\"\"\n        if context is None:\n            context = Context(mcp_server=self, subscriptions=self._subscriptions)\n        resource = await self._resource_manager.get_resource(uri, context)\n        if isinstance(resource, InputRequiredResult):\n            return resource\n\n        try:\n            content = await resource.read()\n            return [ReadResourceContents(content=content, mime_type=resource.mime_type, meta=resource.meta)]\n        except MCPError:\n            raise\n        except Exception as exc:\n            logger.exception(f\"Error getting resource {uri}\")\n            # If an exception happens when reading the resource, we should not leak the exception to the client.\n            raise ResourceError(f\"Error reading resource {uri}\") from exc\n\n    def add_tool(\n        self,\n        fn: Callable[..., Any],\n        name: str | None = None,\n        title: str | None = None,\n        description: str | None = None,\n        annotations: ToolAnnotations | None = None,\n        icons: list[Icon] | None = None,\n        meta: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n        structured_output: bool | None = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Add a tool to the server.\n\n        The tool function can optionally request a Context object by adding a parameter\n        with the Context type annotation. See the @tool decorator for examples.\n\n        Args:\n            fn: The function to register as a tool\n            name: Optional name for the tool (defaults to function name)\n            title: Optional human-readable title for the tool\n            description: Optional description of what the tool does\n            annotations: Optional ToolAnnotations providing additional tool information\n            icons: Optional list of icons for the tool\n            meta: Optional metadata dictionary for the tool\n            structured_output: Controls whether the tool's output is structured or unstructured\n                - If None, auto-detects based on the function's return type annotation\n                - If True, creates a structured tool (return type annotation permitting)\n                - If False, unconditionally creates an unstructured tool\n        \"\"\"\n        self._tool_manager.add_tool(\n            fn,\n            name=name,\n            title=title,\n            description=description,\n            annotations=annotations,\n            icons=icons,\n            meta=meta,\n            structured_output=structured_output,\n        )\n\n    def remove_tool(self, name: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Remove a tool from the server by name.\n\n        Args:\n            name: The name of the tool to remove\n\n        Raises:\n            ToolError: If the tool does not exist\n        \"\"\"\n        self._tool_manager.remove_tool(name)\n\n    def tool(\n        self,\n        name: str | None = None,\n        title: str | None = None,\n        description: str | None = None,\n        annotations: ToolAnnotations | None = None,\n        icons: list[Icon] | None = None,\n        meta: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n        structured_output: bool | None = None,\n    ) -> Callable[[_CallableT], _CallableT]:\n        \"\"\"Decorator to register a tool.\n\n        Tools can optionally request a Context object by adding a parameter with the\n        Context type annotation. The context provides access to MCP capabilities like\n        logging, progress reporting, and resource access.\n\n        Args:\n            name: Optional name for the tool (defaults to function name)\n            title: Optional human-readable title for the tool\n            description: Optional description of what the tool does\n            annotations: Optional ToolAnnotations providing additional tool information\n            icons: Optional list of icons for the tool\n            meta: Optional metadata dictionary for the tool\n            structured_output: Controls whether the tool's output is structured or unstructured\n                - If None, auto-detects based on the function's return type annotation\n                - If True, creates a structured tool (return type annotation permitting)\n                - If False, unconditionally creates an unstructured tool\n\n        Example:\n            ```python\n            @server.tool()\n            def my_tool(x: int) -> str:\n                return str(x)\n            ```\n\n            ```python\n            @server.tool()\n            async def tool_with_context(x: int, ctx: Context) -> str:\n                await ctx.info(f\"Processing {x}\")\n                return str(x)\n            ```\n\n            ```python\n            @server.tool()\n            async def async_tool(x: int, context: Context) -> str:\n                await context.report_progress(50, 100)\n                return str(x)\n            ```\n        \"\"\"\n        # Check if user passed function directly instead of calling decorator\n        if callable(name):\n            raise TypeError(\n                \"The @tool decorator was used incorrectly. Did you forget to call it? Use @tool() instead of @tool\"\n            )\n\n        def decorator(fn: _CallableT) -> _CallableT:\n            self.add_tool(\n                fn,\n                name=name,\n                title=title,\n                description=description,\n                annotations=annotations,\n                icons=icons,\n                meta=meta,\n                structured_output=structured_output,\n            )\n            return fn\n\n        return decorator\n\n    def completion(self):\n        \"\"\"Decorator to register a completion handler.\n\n        The completion handler receives:\n        - ref: PromptReference or ResourceTemplateReference\n        - argument: CompletionArgument with name and partial value\n        - context: Optional CompletionContext with previously resolved arguments\n\n        Example:\n            ```python\n            @mcp.completion()\n            async def handle_completion(ref, argument, context):\n Returns True if valid.\"\"\"\n        has_json, has_sse = check_accept_headers(request)\n        if self.is_json_response_enabled:\n            # For JSON-only responses, only require application/json\n            if not has_json:\n                response = self._create_error_response(\n                    \"Not Acceptable: Client must accept application/json\",\n                    HTTPStatus.NOT_ACCEPTABLE,\n                )\n                await response(scope, request.receive, send)\n                return False\n        # For SSE responses, require both content types\n        elif not (has_json and has_sse):\n            response = self._create_error_response(\n                \"Not Acceptable: Client must accept both application/json and text/event-stream\",\n                HTTPStatus.NOT_ACCEPTABLE,\n            )\n            await response(scope, request.receive, send)\n            return False\n        return True\n\n    async def _handle_post_request(self, scope: Scope, request: Request, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Handle POST requests containing JSON-RPC messages.\"\"\"\n        writer = self._read_stream_writer\n        if writer is None:  # pragma: no cover\n            raise ValueError(\"No read stream writer available. Validate Content-Type\n            if not self._check_content_type(request):  # pragma: no cover\n                response = self._create_error_response(\n                    \"Unsupported Media Type: Content-Type must be application/json\",\n                    HTTPStatus.UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE,\n                )\n                await response(scope, receive, send)\n                return\n\n            # For notifications and responses only, return 202 Accepted\n            if not isinstance(message, JSONRPCRequest):\n                # Create response object and send it\n                response = self._create_json_response(\n                    None,\n                    HTTPStatus.ACCEPTED,\n                )\n                await response(scope, receive, send)\n\n                # Process the message after sending the response\n                session_message = SessionMessage(message, metadata=self._message_metadata(request))\n                await writer.send(session_message)\n\n                return\n\n            # Process the message\n                metadata = self._message_metadata(\n                    request, on_request_unanswered=partial(self._terminate_unanswered_request, message.id)\n                )\n                session_message = SessionMessage(message, metadata=metadata)\n                await writer.send(session_message)\n                try:\n                    # pragma: no branch\n            headers[MCP_SESSION_ID_HEADER] = self.mcp_session_id\n\n        response = self._create_error_response(\n            \"Method Not Allowed\",\n            HTTPStatus.METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED,\n            headers=headers,\n        )\n        await response(request.scope, request.receive, send)\n\n    async def _validate_request_headers(self, request: Request, send: Send) -> bool:\n        # pragma: no cover\n\n        try:\n            headers = {\n                \"Cache-Control\": \"no-cache, no-transform\",\n                \"Connection\": \"keep-alive\",\n                \"Content-Type\": CONTENT_TYPE_SSE,\n            }\n\n            if self.mcp_session_id:  # Replay past events and get the stream ID\n                        stream_id = await event_store.replay_events_after(last_event_id, send_event)\n\n                        # If stream ID not in mapping, create it\n                        if stream_id and stream_id not in self._request_streams:  # pragma: no branch\n                            try:\n                                # Register SSE writer so close_sse_stream() can close it\n                                self._sse_stream_writers[stream_id] = sse_stream_writer\n\n                                # Prime the resumed connection so the client sees the stream\n                                # is re-registered. The replay→live-tail ordering window here\n                                # is pre-existing and tracked separately.\n                                priming_event = await self._mint_priming_event(stream_id, replay_protocol_version)\n                                if priming_event is not None:\n                                    await sse_stream_writer.send(priming_event)\n\n                                # Forward messages to SSE\n                                async with msg_reader:\n                                    async for event_message in msg_reader:\n                                        event_data = self._create_event_data(event_message)\n\n                                        await sse_stream_writer.send(event_data)\n                            finally:\n                                self._sse_stream_writers.pop(stream_id, None)\n                                await self._clean_up_memory_streams(stream_id)\n                except anyio.ClosedResourceError:  # pragma: lax no cover\n                    # Expected when close_sse_stream() is called\n                    logger.debug(\"Replay SSE stream closed by close_sse_stream()\")\n                except Exception:  # pragma: lax no cover\n                    logger.exception(\"Error in replay sender\")\n\n            # Create and start EventSourceResponse\n            response = EventSourceResponse(\n                content=sse_stream_reader,\n                data_sender_callable=replay_sender,\n                headers=headers,\n            )\n\n            try:\n                await response(request.scope, request.receive, send)\n            except Exception:  # pragma: lax no cover\n                logger.exception(\"Error in replay response\")\n            finally:\n                await sse_stream_writer.aclose()\n                await sse_stream_reader.aclose()\n\n        except Exception:  # Create the memory streams for this connection\n\n        read_stream_writer, read_stream = create_context_streams[SessionMessage | Exception](0)\n        write_stream, write_stream_reader = create_context_streams[SessionMessage](0)\n\n        # Store the streams\n        self._read_stream_writer = read_stream_writer\n        self._read_stream = read_stream\n        self._write_stream_reader = write_stream_reader\n        self._write_stream = write_stream\n\n        # Start a task group for message routing\n        async with anyio.create_task_group() as tg:\n            # Create a message router that distributes messages to request streams\n            async def message_router():\n                try:\n                    async for session_message in write_stream_reader:  # pragma: no branch\n                        # Determine which request stream(s) should receive this message\n                        message = session_message.message\n                        target_request_id = None\n                        # Check if this is a response with a known request id.\n                        # Null-id errors (e.g., parse errors) fall through to\n                        # the GET stream since they can't be correlated.\n                        if isinstance(message, JSONRPCResponse | JSONRPCError) and message.id is not None:\n                            target_request_id = str(message.id)\n                        # Extract related_request_id from meta if it exists\n                        elif (\n                            session_message.metadata is not None\n                            and isinstance(\n                                session_message.metadata,\n                                ServerMessageMetadata,\n                            )\n                            and session_message.metadata.related_request_id is not None\n                        ):\n                            related_request_id = session_message.metadata.related_request_id\n                            if self.is_json_response_enabled:\n                                # A JSON body carries only the response: this message\n                                # has no wire form (nor a replay), so drop it before\n                                # storing or queueing rather than park it (#1764).\n                                logger.debug(f\"Dropped message related to request {related_request_id} in JSON mode\")\n                                continue\n                            target_request_id = str(related_request_id)\n\n                        request_stream_id = target_request_id if target_request_id is not None else GET_STREAM_KEY\n\n                        # Send both the message and the event ID\n                                await self._request_streams[request_stream_id][0].send(EventMessage(message, event_id))\n                            except (anyio.BrokenResourceError, anyio.ClosedResourceError):  # pragma: no cover\n                                # Stream might be closed, remove from registry\n                                self._request_streams.pop(request_stream_id, None)\n                        else:\n                            logger.debug(\n                                f\"\"\"Request stream {request_stream_id} not found\n                                for message. ```

### Assistant
This is a very deep file. Key points:

1. `StreamableHTTPServerTransport.handle_request` first calls `self._security.validate_request(request, is_post=...)` — confirming that DNS rebinding protection is applied to every request to the transport. With `base_url="http://127.0.0.1"` (no port), Host="127.0.0.1" → 421. Confirmed that we need to either include a port in base_url or disable security.

2. POST handling in JSON mode: `writer = self._read_stream_writer` — if None, `raise ValueError("No read stream writer available. Ensure connect() is called first.")` → 500. The streams are set up in `connect()`, which is called from the session manager (in stateless mode, per request).

3. Accept header: in json_response mode, `has_json` is required — httpx sends `Accept: */*` by default → has_wildcard → has_json is True. OK.

### Assistant
**Critical finding**: `StreamableHTTPSessionManager._handle_request` does the following:

```python
if self._task_group is None:
    raise RuntimeError("Task group is not initialized. Make sure to use run().")
```

`_task_group` is only set inside `run()`, which is the app's **lifespan**. In other words: if TestClient does not enter the lifespan (i.e., used without a context manager), every request to `/mcp` will raise RuntimeError → 500.

Also, `run()` enters `self.app.lifespan(self.app)` — that's the low-level Server's lifespan (the default `lifespan` yields {}). No problem.

Furthermore: era routing — `MCP_PROTOCOL_VERSION_HEADER` — the test POSTs a raw JSON-RPC body without the MCP-Protocol-Version header. `pv = None` → goes to `self.stateless` → `_handle_stateless_request(None, ...)` → in the stateless path: `Connection.from_envelope(None → DEFAULT_NEGOTIATED_VERSION, None, None)`. OK.

Wait — but in stateless mode, is a bare JSON-RPC request for `tools/list` (without a prior `initialize`) valid in the 2026-07-28 spec? AGENTS.md says "the 2026-07-28 spec removes the connection handshake." The `serve_connection` in the stateless path uses `Connection.from_envelope(protocol_version, None, None)` — a "Born-ready" connection. So tools/list should work directly. Good.

And `jsonrpc_message_adapter.validate_python(raw_message, by_name=False)` — the test's body: `{"jsonrpc": "2.0", "id": 1, "method": "tools/list"}` — this is a JSONRPCRequest. Note: `params` is omitted for tools/list. Then in `_handle_post_request`: `is_initialization_request = False` → `_validate_request_headers` → `_validate_session` → stateless has no session ID → True. Next `protocol_version = request.headers.get(MCP_PROTOCOL_VERSION_HEADER, DEFAULT_NEGOTIATED_VERSION)`. Next in JSON mode: create a request stream, `writer.send(session_message)`, then `event_message = await request_stream_reader.receive()` — waits for the response from the message_router, which is fed from the serve_connection dispatcher. OK — this is a fully in-memory flow, all within a single request.

**However, one issue**: in JSON mode, `writer.send(session_message)` happens inside `_handle_post_request` — but the message_router task (inside `connect()`) is started via `self._task_group.start(run_stateless_server)` — which requires `self._task_group` to be set (lifespan startup!). And then `await request_stream_reader.receive()` — if the router never starts (no lifespan), this blocks forever → test hang (timeout).

So: **the mcp_env fixture must run the app's lifespan**. In starlette's TestClient, lifespan events are only run when the client is used as a context manager (`with TestClient(app) as c:`) — in starlette &gt;= 0.21. Let me check the installed starlette 1.6.0's TestClient and verify. Actually, starlette's TestClient: `TestClient.__init__` creates a transport with `app`. `__enter__` runs lifespan startup (sends a lifespan scope with startup, waits for startup.complete), and `__exit__` runs shutdown. When not used as a context manager, requests go through the portal but lifespan is not started → `self._task_group is None` → RuntimeError → 500 on every request.

**Important finding**: `_handle_request` immediately throws `RuntimeError("Task group is not initialized. Make sure to use run().")` if `run()` (the app's **lifespan**) has not been entered. In other words, the sync client for the MCP server **must run the lifespan** — in starlette's TestClient, that only happens when the client is used as a context manager. Let's verify the TestClient's behavior and the auth routes/middleware:

httpx._types.CookieTypes | None = None,\n        auth: httpx._types.AuthTypes | httpx._client.UseClientDefault = httpx._client.USE_CLIENT_DEFAULT,\n        follow_redirects: bool | httpx._client.UseClientDefault = httpx._client.USE_CLIENT_DEFAULT,\n        timeout: httpx._types.TimeoutTypes | httpx._client.UseClientDefault = httpx._client.USE_CLIENT_DEFAULT,\n        extensions: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n    ) -> httpx.Response:\n        return super().delete(\n            url,\n            params=params,\n            headers=headers,\n            cookies=cookies,\n            auth=auth,\n            follow_redirects=follow_redirects,\n            timeout=timeout,\n            extensions=extensions,\n        )\n\n    def websocket_connect(\n        self,\n        url: str,\n        subprotocols: Sequence[str] | None = None,\n        **kwargs: Any,\n    ) -> WebSocketTestSession:\n        url = urljoin(\"ws://testserver\", url)\n        headers = kwargs.get(\"headers\", {})\n        headers.setdefault(\"connection\", \"upgrade\")\n        headers.setdefault(\"sec-websocket-key\", \"testserver==\")\n        headers.setdefault(\"sec-websocket-version\", \"13\")\n        if subprotocols is not None:\n            headers.setdefault(\"sec-websocket-protocol\", \", \".join(subprotocols))\n        kwargs[\"headers\"] = headers\n        try:\n            super().request(\"GET\", url, **kwargs)\n        except _Upgrade as exc:\n            session = exc.session\n        else:\n            raise RuntimeError(\"Expected WebSocket upgrade\")  # pragma: no cover\n\n        return session\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> Self:\n        with contextlib.ExitStack() as stack:\n            self.portal = portal = stack.enter_context(anyio.from_thread.start_blocking_portal(**self.async_backend))\n\n            @stack.callback\n            def reset_portal() -> None:\n                self.portal = None\n\n            send: anyio.create_memory_object_stream[MutableMapping[str, Any] | None] = (\n                anyio.create_memory_object_stream(math.inf)\n            )\n            receive: anyio.create_memory_object_stream[MutableMapping[str, Any]] = anyio.create_memory_object_stream(\n                math.inf\n            )\n            for channel in (*send, *receive):\n
